#2917. Padres’ 38-Save Closer Has a Hidden Edge in Reliever of the Year Race

SAN DIEGO, CALIFORNIA - OCTOBER 07: Mason Miller #22 of the San Diego Padres prepares to pitch against the Milwaukee Brewers during the seventh inning in game four of the National League Division Series at Petco Park on October 07, 2026 in San Diego, California. (Photo by Orlando Ramirez/Getty Images) Getty

SAN DIEGO, CALIFORNIA - SEPTEMBER 30: Freddy Fermin #54 and Mason Miller #22 of the San Diego Padres shake hands after the victory against the Chicago Cubs in game two of the National League Wild Card Series at Petco Park on September 30, 2026 in San Diego, California. (Photo by Orlando Ramirez/Getty Images) Getty

Mason Miller finished the 2026 regular season with 38 saves for the San Diego Padres. His strongest argument for National League Reliever of the Year comes from a different part of his record. Among the three finalists, he created the widest gap between how often he struck hitters out and how often he walked them.

The Baseball Writers' Association of America listed Miller, Philadelphia's Jhoan Duran and Milwaukee's Trevor Megill as finalists. The winner will be announced November 11. Writers voted before the postseason, so the comparison starts with each pitcher's regular-season work. Miller's case comes down to how often he finished plate appearances himself, and how much work he took on over the full season.

GettyMason Miller of the San Diego Padres prepares to pitch against the Milwaukee Brewers in Game 4 of the National League Division Series at Petco Park in San Diego on October 7, 2026. (Photo by Orlando Ramirez/Getty Images)

Miller's pitching line shows 129 strikeouts and 26 walks against 272 batters. Subtracting his walks from his strikeouts and dividing by batters faced produces a 37.9% strikeout-minus-walk rate. The calculation puts pitchers with different workloads on the same scale.

Duran struck out 84 and walked 10 in 236 matchups, a 31.4% rate by the same calculation. Megill struck out 69 and walked 15 against 242 hitters, coming in at 22.3%. Miller led Duran by 6.5 percentage points.

Duran walked only 4.2% of the hitters he faced, well below Miller's 9.6%. That is a significant advantage when protecting a tight lead. Miller's 47.4% strikeout rate, however, gave him enough separation to lead the group even after accounting for the extra walks.

Padres' 21-Homer Free Agent Has a 46-Point Gap in His Reunion Case

GettySan Diego Padres teammates Freddy Fermin and Mason Miller shake hands after a win over the Chicago Cubs in Game 2 of the National League Wild Card Series at Petco Park in San Diego on September 30, 2026. (Photo by Orlando Ramirez/Getty Images)

The rate comparison matters more alongside the volume. Miller pitched 70 innings. Duran worked 59.1, and Megill reached 61.2. San Diego got the strongest strikeout-minus-walk performance among the finalists across the largest workload of the three.

Each competitor brings a different strength. Duran finished with a 1.82 ERA and allowed two homers. Megill allowed slightly fewer walks and hits per inning than Miller, with a 0.89 WHIP against Miller's 0.90. Miller combined his strikeout advantage with the lowest ERA of the group at 1.67.

Duran also converted 33 of 35 save chances, a 94.3% success rate. Miller converted 38 of 41, or 92.7%. That gives Duran a slight edge in conversion and makes Miller's advantage against individual hitters the more revealing comparison for the award.

Save totals reflect the leads a team creates and the situations a manager hands its closer. Strikeout and walk rates offer a separate way to assess what the pitcher did after taking the mound. That helps distinguish performances even when all three pitchers spent the season handling late innings.

On this comparison, Miller would get my vote. His command left some room for improvement, and the other finalists made compelling cases in specific areas. Miller made up the difference with an exceptional ability to finish at-bats, and the Padres trusted him with more innings than either rival logged.

#2914. Toto Wolff responds to George Russell's angry radio messages in Brazilian GP qualifying

Mercedes team principal Toto Wolff has addressed George Russell's furious team radio messages during qualifying for the Brazilian Grand Prix.

The British driver finished second in the rain-soaked session at Interlagos behind McLaren's Lando Norris. The Brackley outfit told him to back off midway through his last push lap in Q3 as more rain approached and Alex Albon's Williams was ahead of him on track.

At the time, Russell fumed over the team radio when it became clear the rain was too heavy to continue with the session.

"Mate, you told me to back off! What the hell?!" he said on the team radio. Wolff responded to tell Russell he was P2, but that did not satisfy the driver. "P2. I don't want to be P2. I want to be P1."

Russell has received a five-place grid penalty for the Brazilian Grand Prix after his Mercedes was fitted with a fresh internal combustion engine, so will start the race from seventh.

Speaking to Sky Sports F1 after qualifying, Wolff explained there was no way of knowing what the best decision was at the time.

"If we were to have a crystal ball to know whether you should back off or not, that would be helpful," Wolff explained.

"It was a difficult one, a 50:50 call. He was running into Albon. He was 18 milliseconds up on Norris at that stage. I don't think it would have been enough for pole, but he drove perfectly all session and merited to be on pole.

"He deserved it, but you can't make that call. It's 50% right or wrong. George was in a league of his own today."

Putting his frustrations to one side, Russell said he felt confident in his Mercedes machinery.

"It depends on the weather. I felt good out there," the Briton said in his televised post-qualifying interview.

"It's the first time in a while I've felt confident in the car. I felt more confident in the wet than I do the dry. I don't really understand why that was. I felt more like myself, was in the fight for pole every single lap. Unfortunately, when it mattered, we didn't execute."

#2910. ‘I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers

'I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown has spent the opening weeks of the season describing the Sixers as a work in progress.

He offered a similarly humble perspective Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena. His performance, however, provided the clearest look yet at how he can influence this group while sharing the floor with the club's other established stars.

Brown finished with 25 points, 11 rebounds and five assists in the Sixers' 126-107 win over the Chicago Bulls. He shot 9 for 15 from the field, made four three-pointers and did not commit a turnover.

Five of his rebounds came on the offensive end, an area Brown has emphasized while the Sixers continue piecing together their frontcourt rotation.

"Just a mindset," Brown said. "Just trying to create extra opportunities for my team. As we're trying to patch together our fives and our front line, I try to make an extra contribution and emphasis on the glass."

Brown has also been careful about how he describes his place on a roster that already belonged to Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey.

Maxey scored 15 of his 26 points in the fourth quarter Monday, continuing his torrid start to the season. He has led the Sixers in scoring while being the only starter to appear in all eight games.

Brown is not interested in interrupting that.

"Maxey's been leading the way, 100 percent," Brown said. "He's been here. This is his team. Him and Joel have been here for a while, so the mindset has been to complement those guys. I'm finding my footing, but Maxey has been leading the way for sure."

Brown's footing looked firm against Chicago.

Beyond his scoring, he helped the Sixers control the glass, moved the ball within an offense that generated 23 assists during the first half and supplied the physicality Nick Nurse expects from him.

"The dude is tough and he wins," Nurse said. "I'll take that all day long. The degree of difficulty on some of the shots he hit was really amazing. I thought he pounded the glass and got some key rebounds. Makes a lot of winning plays. Man, he's a winner."

Brown spent the first 10 years of his career in Boston. He won a championship there, reached another NBA Finals and grew from a 20-year-old prospect into one of the league's premier two-way wings.

Moving into a new system alongside James, Embiid and Maxey was never going to be seamless.

"I mean, he's fitting in along with all of us," James said. "We're getting better and better. It's not going to happen overnight. He spent his first 10 years in a system in Boston, in the city, so it's going to take some adjustment.

"It takes adjustment for him. It's going to take adjustment for the rest of the guys that joined this franchise as well. But tonight was a great step."

James pointed to Brown's "pressure, his shot-making ability, his rebounding ability" and his professionalism.

Brown's early influence has extended beyond his production.

The Sixers entered Monday with one of the NBA's best half-court defenses. Brown has been one of the group's most persistent voices, stressing communication, preparation and attention to opposing players' tendencies.

"That's the part that I care about the most as a group, not individually," Brown said. "Having a mentality night in and night out, respecting your opponent, respecting the game, making sure that we are executing our coverages, we're being clear in our communication and then we're also taking away tendencies on a night-to-night basis.

"Those things I just explained are over 50 percent of the battle."

Brown understands that maintaining those details across an 82-game schedule will require more than reminders.

"A little bit of accountability," he said. "You learn different guys require different things. How you communicate with each guy may be a little different, but accountability for sure and confrontation if need be.

"The offense is going to be what it's going to be, but our defense has got to be non-negotiable. That's something I say all the time. Non-negotiable. You want to be a good team, defense is non-negotiable."

The Sixers showed both sides of their defensive potential Monday.

They built a double-digit lead during the first half, allowed Chicago to score 36 points in the second quarter and then created separation after halftime. Philadelphia held the Bulls to 20 points in the third quarter before pulling away in the fourth.

Brown did not celebrate their ability to raise their intensity after it slipped.

"I don't like that we have that," he said. "It's good that we have shown the potential, but we've got to keep that switch on. It doesn't work like that where you just flip the switch.

"It can catch up to us and it can bite us against a better team or on a night that we're not being as crisp on offense. It can't be a switch just because we're playing a lesser opponent or whatever the case may be. We've got to make sure that mentality is from the beginning to end. Can't skip any steps."

That urgency accompanies considerable optimism.

Brown said the Sixers enjoy playing together. He loves when James grabs a rebound because "he's like a quarterback," capable of launching an outlet pass or immediately pushing the pace.

The group is learning when to attack, when to move the ball and how to avoid hesitating in its effort to play unselfishly.

"It's a lot of talent, man," Brown said. "We like each other, and we're just kind of building that chemistry of how we can get this thing to a well-oiled machine. I think we're taking steps forward every single day."

The Sixers are 7-1 despite Brown believing those steps remain incomplete. They have won close games, erased sizable deficits and received important contributions from different players.

Brown's performance Monday showed another version of how their pieces can fit.

"We still have a long way to go to being a really, really solid, great team," Brown said. "But we've been able to win some games. We started out the season great, 7-1, and you haven't seen our best version yet.

#2907. Yankees’ 35-HR Anthony Volpe Replacement Is Within Arm’s Reach

Blue Jays predicted to sign Brandon Lowe. Getty

PITTSBURGH, PENNSYLVANIA - SEPTEMBER 23: Brandon Lowe #5 of the Pittsburgh Pirates watches his hit go for a solo home run during the second inning against the St. Louis Cardinals at PNC Park on September 23, 2026 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania. (Photo by Joe Sargent/Getty Images) Getty

PITTSBURGH, PENNSYLVANIA - AUGUST 19: Brandon Lowe #5 of the Pittsburgh Pirates celebrates with teammates in the dugout after coming around to score on an RBI single by Oneil Cruz #15 in the first inning during the game against the Detroit Tigers at PNC Park on August 19, 2026 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania. (Photo by Justin Berl/Getty Images) Getty

PITTSBURGH, PENNSYLVANIA - AUGUST 14: Brandon Lowe #5 of the Pittsburgh Pirates rounds the bases after hitting a three-run home run in the fifth inning during the game against the Boston Red Sox at PNC Park on August 14, 2026 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania. (Photo by Justin Berl/Getty Images) Getty

The New York Yankees haven't had the success they hoped for with Anthony Volpe this season.

Consequently, a variety of rumors are pushing the 25-year-old infielder off the table.

A few notable names could be viable replacements, but one in particular is surfacing again.

GettyPHOENIX, ARIZONA – MAY 07: Brandon Lowe #5 of the Pittsburgh Pirates rounds the bases after hitting a solo home run against the Arizona Diamondbacks during the first inning of the MLB game at Chase Field on May 07, 2026 in Phoenix, Arizona. (Photo by Christian Petersen/Getty Images)

The Pittsburgh Pirates extended the $23.15 qualifying offer to Brandon Lowe on Nov. 2.

New York still needs a replacement for Volpe, and 32-year-old Lowe fits the bill.

According to Brendan Kuty of The Athletic, he's still worth pursuing.

"The Yankees seem likely to part ways with free agent Jazz Chisholm Jr., and though they liked what Anthony Volpe showed defensively in a limited audition at the position late in the year, they should aim for more offense," Kuty wrote.

"Lowe, 32, would be instant offense. The lefty hitter clubbed 35 home runs last season for the Pittsburgh Pirates, racking up a 121 OPS+ — even though he strikes out a lot (189 K's in 584 at-bats). Lowe also received the qualifying offer, and though he put up a strong 13 Defensive Runs Saved last year, he had a mark of minus-14 in 2025."

GettyPITTSBURGH, PENNSYLVANIA – SEPTEMBER 23: Brandon Lowe #5 of the Pittsburgh Pirates watches his hit go for a solo home run during the second inning against the St. Louis Cardinals at PNC Park on September 23, 2026 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania. (Photo by Joe Sargent/Getty Images)

He should remain a top target in free agency for the Yankees.

GettyPITTSBURGH, PENNSYLVANIA – AUGUST 19: Brandon Lowe #5 of the Pittsburgh Pirates celebrates with teammates in the dugout after coming around to score on an RBI single by Oneil Cruz #15 in the first inning during the game against the Detroit Tigers at PNC Park on August 19, 2026 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania. (Photo by Justin Berl/Getty Images)

Lowe's 2026 campaign marked his ninth season in the big leagues, but only his first with the Pirates.

He landed with Pittsburgh after the Tampa Bay Rays traded him to the Pirates in a three-team trade back in December 2025.

During his latest — and first campaign — in Pittsburgh, he slashed .260/.331/.493 with an .824 OPS and 35 homers through 150 games.

He hit 29 doubles, one triple and 96 RBIs by the end of the regular season

GettyPITTSBURGH, PENNSYLVANIA – AUGUST 14: Brandon Lowe #5 of the Pittsburgh Pirates rounds the bases after hitting a three-run home run in the fifth inning during the game against the Boston Red Sox at PNC Park on August 14, 2026 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania. (Photo by Justin Berl/Getty Images)

At the time of this writing, Lowe is a Silver Slugger Award finalist at second base.

Having this level of production at the plate would be incredibly valuable for New York.

He can be rather underrated compared to other free agents, but he shouldn't be overlooked.

#2906. Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30

Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers remain the Eastern Conference's sole leader.

They earned a 112-106 road win over the Pistons on Wednesday night to move to 8-1 on the season and stay at the top of the standings. Detroit fell to 6-2.

The Sixers last started 8-1 in the 2023-24 season, which was Nick Nurse's first as the team's head coach.

Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 30 points and pulled down seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid had 26 points and a season-best 15 rebounds. LeBron James posted 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. Tyrese Maxey added 19 points and seven assists.

The Pistons' leading scorers were Duncan Robinson with 27 points and Cade Cunningham with 26.

The Sixers were down Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pistons' injured players included Jalen Duren (right hamstring strain) and Isaiah Joe (right hip contusion).

Nurse told reporters in Detroit he thinks "it'll be another 10 to 12 days before they kind of clear (Barlow) for getting on the court to see where he is," according to Sixers Wire's Ky Carlin.

The Sixers will visit the Cavaliers on Friday night. Here are observations on their victory over Detroit:

Embiid assisted the Sixers' first basket with a sharp pass out of a double team. He saw Brown's cut along the baseline and dished to him for a layup.

Detroit ran several double drag screen actions early, forcing Embiid to defend on the perimeter. The Pistons started 7 for 11 from the floor and took a 17-11 edge on a Robinson three-pointer.

The Sixers then made a 10-0 run and Embiid was right in the middle of it. He converted a put-back layup after a pretty high-low feed from James. Embiid hit two foul shots to give the Sixers the lead. The team raised its defensive level and Detroit's offense grew frigid.

Embiid was very good on the glass in the first half, grabbing eight rebounds. The Sixers had an excellent rebounding game as a team, limiting the Pistons to four offensive boards over 48 minutes.

Embiid didn't break out of his early-season three-point shooting slump, going 0 for 3. He's 3 for 26 (11.5 percent) on the season and hasn't made a three since the Sixers' second game.

Late in the second quarter, Embiid got into a dead-ball dust-up with Tolu Smith. After an officials' review, Embiid was assessed a technical foul for "initiating" the altercation.

Playing on his 26th birthday, Maxey missed his first four field goals and was scoreless until he hit a floater with 31.5 seconds left in the first quarter.

Brown picked up the scoring slack, tallying 10 points in the first on 4-for-6 shooting and hitting a deep, buzzer-beating three to put the Sixers up 29-21. He produced at all three levels, chose smart spots to attack and looked comfortable in the rhythm of the Sixers' offense.

Meanwhile, James did his usuall stellar work as a passer. He assisted two Adem Bona slams in the second quarter and constantly spotted open teammates. Nurse rewarded Bona's strong opening stint, playing him for a lengthy stretch.

Brown swished a corner triple to extend the Sixers' lead to 48-31. With a 3-for-5 night, Brown is at 45.7 percent from long distance so far this season. It wouldn't be surprising at all if Brown's three-point shooting improved from last season's 34.7 percent clip. With such an abundance of star power, the Sixers' hope will be that everyone gets regular open looks beyond the arc throughout the year.

As for Maxey, he sat with 3:26 left in the second quarter after being whistled for his third foul. Even with a mere two points from Maxey, the Sixers entered halftime holding a 14-point advantage.

The Pistons played better basketball to begin the third quarter.

Following a John Collins alley-oop jam, Nurse called timeout with his team's lead down to single digits. The Pistons' offense sustained some success, but the Sixers prevented a sustained run.

Embiid played an especially physical, aggressive third quarter. He tipped in his own miss and Maxey then sunk a three to make the Sixers' lead 84-68. They were up as many as 17 points in the third.

Embiid sat out the fourth quarter Monday night for the Sixers' win over the Bulls and said he was on a minutes restriction (25) because of his right foot plantar fasciitis. He played the fourth period against the Pistons and logged 33 minutes. Bona went back to the locker room early in the fourth. Nurse told reporters postgame that Bona had "neck soreness."

Detroit did not let the Sixers coast to victory.

Robinson drained a three. Cunningham made a pair of free throws and drove in for a layup that cut the Pistons' deficit to 106-102.

The Sixers halted the Pistons' run before anything disastrous was on the table. Brown blocked a Cunningham three-point try, Embiid knocked down two foul shots, and the East's No. 1 early-season team improved to 4-0 on the road.

#2905. Saints rookie WR Jordyn Tyson plans to make debut vs. Browns after missing first 7 games with hamstring injury: Report

The delayed debut of New Orleans Saints rookie wide receiver Jordyn Tyson is reportedly right around the corner.

Tyson, this year's No. 8 overall pick who missed the Saints' first seven games with a right hamstring injury, plans to play Sunday against the Cleveland Browns, according to NFL insider Jordan Schultz.

The former Arizona State standout "should be good to go," Schultz reported Saturday.

Tyson's latest hamstring setback occurred in August during a training camp practice. He left that joint session with the Jacksonville Jaguars early once his run after the catch ended with an awkward fall near the sideline.

Saints head coach Kellen Moore told reporters that Tyson's foot slipped out from under him as he was hit from behind. Moore clarified that the issue wasn't related to the receiver's injury track record.

"I think my understanding of this one, any player that was put in his situation where your foot slips and you get shoved from behind, this has nothing to do with Jordyn's history," Moore said at the time, per ESPN.

"Any player in that situation probably has the same diagnosis coming out of this."

Notably, Tyson missed four games last season at Arizona State with multiple hamstring problems, and his injury obstacles affected his pre-draft process.

Still, the Saints selected him in the top 10.

Tyson began his college career at Colorado, where he teased his tantalizing potential by averaging 21.4 yards per reception and housing a punt return as a freshman in 2022.

Funny enough, that special teams score came against his future school, Arizona State, on a day when he also reeled in five passes for 115 yards and another TD.

In 2024, Tyson broke out with the Sun Devils, helping them reach the College Football Playoff and finished with 75 catches, 1,101 receiving yards and 10 touchdowns. Even during a shortened 2025 campaign, he totaled nine TDs, with one of them arriving on the ground.

Staying on the field has been a challenge for the 6-foot-2, 203-pound Tyson. Down the stretch of his lone go-around at Colorado, he suffered a torn ACL, MCL and PCL. He missed the end of a memorable 2024 season with the Sun Devils due to a broken collarbone that sidelined him for the Big 12 title game and the CFP.

But he's thought to have WR1 upside, given his ball skills — including his knack for making contested catches — and his ability to play every receiver spot on the field with impressive quickness.

At the moment, the 2-5 Saints are tied for third in the league with 39 pass attempts per game. Without a reliable run game, QB Tyler Shough is having to chuck the ball around the yard.

Having Tyson at his disposal should help.

#2902. LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it

LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James is one of kind in so many ways. What he did Monday night might be the cherry on top.

In the third quarter of the Sixers' 126-107 victory over the Bulls, James decided he'd take fan interaction to a whole new level.

James looked for a transition pass from Tyrese Maxey, who snagged a rebound. James was behind the pass and his momentum took him into the stands, where he disappeared for a few moments.

It wasn't until another angle was shown on replay that we could see what actually went down in the darkness of the seats. James didn't hold back giving reporters a play-by-play.

"I spilled some of his wine," he said postgame. "I crashed into him, spilled some of his wine. So I wanted to see if it was worth it on my arm and how good it was. It was alright."

Troy Clemons – the Sixers fan whose drink spilled – explained his perspective on the interaction with James to Kris Rhim of ESPN.

"[I was] walking to my seat … LeBron crashed into me, he grabbed me, he said 'My bad,' my drink spilled everywhere. He took my drink from me and he basically just took a sip of it, gave it back and ran back on the [court]."

"At the end of the day he's another man like me," Clemons said. "I mean he's LeBron, but at the end of the day, I threw that cup in the trash. And it's nothing up with him, I just got a fresh drink though."

It should be noted that players are not supposed to drink from fans' cups, but it's rare for the NBA to fine someone for a spontaneous interaction such as this. In 2019, a similar thing happened when Mike Scott tumbled into the Bucks' court-side seats. The Sixers star jokingly took a sip of a fan's beverage and the NBA opted not to fine him. Time will tell if James has the same fate.

If nothing else, an interaction like this just shows how much fun James seems to be having.

The 24-year veteran noted the importance of finding joy in the game as he grinds through the season.

"It's a long marathon. And if you don't find ways to have fun throughout it, even though you're working and even though you're locked in, then it could wear on you. So for me, especially at this point in my career, being able to just appreciate the moments, the small moments, these fans are great to us. We try to be great to them as well. By playing the game the right way, playing the game hard and then also just being showmen as well, it was a great game for us."

Head coach Nick Nurse also weighed in on the 41-year-old's mindset coming into the season.

"This summer I think he probably stated publicly that he wanted to go some place and have some fun playing. He loves the game, [it's his] 24th year. It's a group of guys, like I keep saying, that have been super focused in the intent of playing the right way. I think he's a big part of that."

The vibes are high. It's difficult for them not to be.

With Monday's win against the Bulls, the Sixers are 7-1 and lead the Eastern Conference. They've won four in a row and the only team they've lost to is the Pistons. The Sixers will face them on Wednesday to start a three-game road trip.

#2900. 5 questions with Silver and Black Pride: Is there any hope for the Raiders’ defense against the 49ers?

Matt Holder from Silver and Black Pride helped us out this week with five questions about this week's matchup between the San Francisco 49ers and the Las Vegas Raiders.

We talked about Kyle Shanahan and Kirk Cousins, the Raiders' defense against the Niners' offense, Las Vegas's running back situation, and Brock Bowers.

1) You can't tell the Kirk Cousins story without Kyle Shanahan. How surprising is it to see Cousins play this well, and do you think he'll continue that this season, even if the Raiders remain one-dimensional?

Heading into the season, I thought Cousins was going to be nothing more than a placeholder for Fernando Mendoza. So, I'm very shocked by how well he's been playing and was dead wrong in the offseason. He's one of the biggest reasons the Raiders are in the playoff picture at the halfway point of the season.

You're 100% correct that Vegas' offense is one-dimensional, and the lack of a running game could lead to the 38-year-old starting to show his age down the stretch. But so far, there's been no sign of regression from Cousins. He isn't perfect; his age/lack of mobility and arm strength show up when under pressure, but he's coming off his best game of the year last week against the Jets.

2) Is there any hope for the Raiders defense? Everybody scores against them. The 49ers won't have Mike Evans, but it still seems like the Niners will move the ball up and down the field. How do you see the Raiders getting stops?

Kyle Shanahan's game plan can be pretty simple this week: line up in 12 or 13 personnel (or technically 21/22 personnel with Kyle Juszczyk) and pound the rock. Shanahan doesn't even need Evans because Las Vegas' defense can't stop the run out of those formations.

Even the Jets had success with that, as Braelon Allen averaged 7.0 yards per carry and set a career-high rushing yardage total in the first half. New York is just poorly coached and only ran the ball four times in the second half despite playing with a lead, allowing Vegas to come back in the fourth quarter.

Also, the Raiders' defense doesn't have a legitimate answer for George Kittle. Not that tight ends have necessarily hurt them in the passing game all year, but Kittle is the best one the Raiders' defense has faced, and the unit doesn't have someone who I think can match up with him.

Ultimately, I think San Francisco should have no problem putting up at least 30 points on Sunday. Vegas hasn't kept an opponent under 30 since September, and the Saints put up 27 points in three quarters in Week 3.

3) What's going on at running back? Do you think it was a mistake to take Jeanty? Did he already lose his job? Is it more about Mike Washington being good or Jeanty being bad?

ESPN's Dan Graziano reported this week that Jeanty getting two carries while Washington had 25 in New York was meant to serve as a "wake-up call" for the 2025 No. 6 overall pick. In other words, a 'soft benching'. Also, Jeanty stupidly made a bulletin-board-material comment about the Jets' defense — essentially saying they don't like to tackle — so I think Klint Kubiak's decision was at least partly punitive.

Additionally, the Raiders' running game has been terrible this season, as it was a year ago. Kubiak has tried different offensive line combinations, so he flipped the running back rotation to see how that works. Washington definitely didn't run away with the starting job, but the offense was much better at staying on schedule with him toting the rock.

The rookie doesn't have great vision and will miss opportunities for big plays. But at least last week, he did a better job of getting what he can compared to Jeanty. Moving forward, I expect Washington to continue having a bigger workload, but I think the touches will be closer to 50/50.

Personally, I'm not ready to give up on Jeanty. However, if another team was interested in giving up, say, a second-round pick for him either by Tuesday or in the offseason, I'd probably take that deal. He isn't the biggest problem; the offensive line is, but he isn't helping make the situation better.

4) Klint Kubiak was on the 49ers coaching staff a few years back. How do Raiders fans feel about Kubiak, and is there a real sense that he can lead this team to the playoffs in the near future?

Some in Raider Nation are frustrated with Kubiak because the team started 3-0, giving some hope for the playoffs, only to see a four-game losing streak against playoff-caliber opponents in October. But I'd say those people are in the minority.

Anyone who was sensible knew that this was going to be a rebuilding year for the Raiders, and they already have more wins in 2026 than 2025. That's progress for an organization that had the No. 1 overall pick in the draft last April, especially since the No. 1 pick has barely seen the field.

Sure, the Silver and Black haven't beaten a quality opponent and are "frauds" when it comes to being a postseason contender this season. However, the team is headed in the right direction by separating itself from the bottom of the league.

Overall, I think the majority of the fanbase is happy with Kubiak and feels he's capable of leading them to the playoffs in the future. Again, this was supposed to be year one of a two-year rebuild, and the fact the Raiders are even in the postseason discussion at this stage makes it seem like the plan is ahead of schedule.

5) Is Brock Bowers better than George Kittle? Which skill players would you take over Bowers at this point?

I know a trap question when I see one, LOL!

Right now, I think Kittle is the better overall tight end since he's basically a sixth offensive lineman in the running game. But to me, Bowers is the NFL's most unique weapon in the passing game. He can beat both defensive backs and linebackers by getting open or making ridiculous contested catches and is used more like a 'WR1' than a typical tight end, commanding the attention of the opposing defense's best coverage defender every week. Bowers creates a lot of matchup problems, similar to how Sean Payton used to use Jimmy Graham in New Orleans.

To be honest, there aren't many other skill players around the league that I'd take over the third-year offensive weapon. It'd have to be one of the top-tier wide receivers, like a Ja'Marr Chase or Justin Jefferson type. Bowers is just such a unicorn that it'd take a lot for me to want to give him up.

#2898. Bebé completes CD Teruel move 16 years after £7.4m Manchester United transfer

Former Manchester United attacker Bebé has officially joined Spanish club CD Teruel for the remainder of the 2026/27 season.

Bebé joined Ibiza in February 2025 after mutually terminating his Racing Ferrol contract, only five months after arriving at the Galician club. He ended that campaign with six goals and four assists from 18 appearances.

Ibiza renewed his deal in June 2025, but the forward confirmed in May 2026 that his contract was expiring and said his representative would take care of his future once the season had finished (via Flashscore).

The 36-year-old subsequently became a free agent. He was already training at Teruel's Pinilla base by Wednesday, with the club considering whether to add him to their squad (via Diario de Huesca).

Teruel have now completed the move, with Bebé registered as a first-team player for the 2026/27 campaign (via COPE).

The Spanish outlet highlighted the Cape Verde international's positional flexibility, stating that he "can play on both wings as well as through the middle of the attack".

United signed Bebé from Vitória Guimarães in August 2010 after paying his £7.4 million release clause. The move – championed by superagent Jorge Mendes, came only weeks after he had joined Vitória from Estrela da Amadora. Sir Alex Ferguson famously admitted to never having seen him play before he signed him.

He made seven competitive appearances for the Red Devils and scored twice, finding the net in the League Cup and Champions League. His two substitute appearances in the Premier League came during United's title-winning 2010/11 campaign.

Bebé later had loan spells with Beşiktaş, Rio Ave and Paços de Ferreira. Benfica bought him for £2.4 million in July 2014 after he was omitted from United's pre-season tour of the United States.

His subsequent Spanish clubs included Córdoba, Rayo Vallecano, Eibar, Real Zaragoza, Racing Ferrol and Ibiza. After representing Portugal at youth level, he made his senior Cape Verde debut in March 2022 and scored against Guadeloupe.

Having already trained with Teruel before signing, Bebé is likely to be integrated quickly into the first-team squad.

Bebé has joined CD Teruel as a free agent for the 2026/27 season.

The former United forward can operate out wide or through the middle.

He had already trained at Pinilla before the move was completed.

#2896. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2892. Arizona Cardinals activate OL Isaiah Adams, make other roster moves

The Arizona Cardinals made some roster moves on Saturday to prepare for their game on Sunday against the Seattle Seahawks. They get a starting offensive lineman back.

The Cardinals activated guard Isaiah Adams from injured reserve. He practiced this week and will return to the starting lineup at right guard.

With that activation, the Cardinals remain at 52 players on the 53-man roster.

They also elevated two players from the practice squad, adding tight end Shawn Bowman and running back Jerome Ford to the roster for the game.

Tackle Jayden Williams has been ruled out with a knee injury. Josh Fryar will likely start at left tackle in his place. With Adams returning to the lineup and with guard Jon Gaines ruled out again with knee and ankle injuries, the Cardinals' backup linemen available will be Hayden Conner, Kingsley Eguakun, Jedrick Wills and Albert Reese. Reese has been inactive as a healthy scratch since he was claimed off waivers.

The Cardinals and Seahawks kick off on Sunday at 4:25 p.m. ET (2:25 p.m. Arizona time) at Lumen Field.

#2888. Chicago Bears Question of the Day: Which teams scare you?

The Chicago Bears are hitting a fever pitch at the moment. A year and a half of Ben Johnson has produced a lot of quality football, but in consecutive weeks the Bears have confidently disposed of the two teams playing in the 2025 Super Bowl.

The addition of Jeffery Simmons, improvement of Caleb Williams, and a litany of talent on both sides of the ball have finally earned the Bears some serious respect across the league. As noted in Sam's article earlier this week, the Bears are being ranked by various outlets anywhere from #7 to #2. They are being viewed as a legitimate title threat.

Still, the NFL's product is based in parity and chaos. There are still a ton of teams that can win the Super Bowl this season. However, that doesn't mean that they are all scary to an Bears fanbase growing in confidence. Halloween has come and gone, but scary season is still upon us. Which leads me to my question.

I think I can sort these teams into two different categories. Rationally scary and irrationally scary.

This is a short list. These are the teams that I think are as good or better than the Bears right now and could beat them in a 1-on-1 matchup.

San Francisco 49ers: The Niners are still the best team in football in my eyes. Shanahan remains the gold standard for head coaching, and he has morphed Brock Purdy into the perfect on-field extension of him. They are talented, fast, smart, and can beat you in a ton of ways. They are the best in the league.

Buffalo Bills: The Bills are elite and will remain elite so long as they have the best QB in the league, Josh Allen, at the helm. Add in talents like Deone Walker and Gregory Rousseau making waves on defense, and the Bills are legit.

Jacksonville Jaguars: I'm not overlooking you, Coach Cohen! The Jags have the 4th-best scoring offense and 2nd-best scoring defense in the league. They're a fantastic team top to bottom that have beaten good teams like the Bengals, Patriots, and Ravens (still good even without Lamar).

Detroit Lions: I think the Lions have a bottom 10 defense in the league. Outside of some talents like Aidan Hutchinson, Alim McNeil, and Brian Branch the defense is going to cost the Lions a lot of games. However, the Bears are 0-2 against the Lions since Ben Johnson has taken over, and that offense is still among the league's best. While I think the Bears are the better team, the Lions still feel like the boogeyman.

Green Bay Packers: Again, the Bears (with QB1) are the better team than the Packers. They are more complete in the trenches, and have a better QB/Coach pairing. Still, watching Jordan Love just heave bombs 40 yards downfield and assume that his WRs would beat our secondary terrifies me. A few broken coverages can change the entire shape of a season; just ask Chris Conte.

Minnesota Vikings: Again…I think the Bears are far and away the more complete team. The Vikings are the inverse of the Lions. Super talented on defense and just wildly inconsistent on offense. Similarly, they have some talents on offense, namely Justin Jefferson, Jordan Addison, TJ Hockenson, and their two tackles, Christian Darrisaw and Brian O'Neil. But the well below average QB play from Kyler Murray has put their ceiling in doubt. As long as Brian Flores is coaching that defense, and KOC is eeking out enough offense, I will have my fears in playing the Vikings.

Let's be clear. The Bears look like they can win it all this season. Even the teams listed above are beatable if the Bears play the way we know they are capable. Stringing together wins against teams like these is another thing. Scary? Maybe a little. But we have Caleb Williams, Jeffery Simmons, and Ben Johnson, so I am sleeping well at night.

#2886. Aidan Mizell's fourth quarter scores lead the way in UCLA's 34-20 win

The UCLA Bruins have done it again. It was far from easy but UCLA picked up yet another victory, knocking off Minnesota 34-20 to move to 9-0 in Bob Chesney's first season with the Bruins.

Minnesota didn't go down easily, forcing a pair of turnovers to stick around in the game. Golden Gophers QB Drake Lindsey began the fourth quarter with a game-tying touchdown pass but from there, it was all UCLA.

UCLA's offense sealed the win by running the same concept on back-to-back plays. On the first play of consecutive drives, the Bruins went deep to wide receiver Aidan Mizell and both times it resulted in 75-yard touchdowns. Mizell has been a terrific deep threat for the Bruins this year, adding a dimension to the offense.

Mizell had the two catches for 150 yards, finding the end zone on both occasions. The two bombs helped out Ty Dieffenbach's numbers, who ended the game going 8-16 through the air with three picks and an interception. There were missed opportunities for the UCLA offense on Saturday but it didn't ultimately cost them. Dieffenbach also added 27 yards on the ground on four carries

It was a solid showing for the UCLA defense but the team didn't generate turnovers like they have for much of the season, getting just one interception in the game. The unit plays a physical brand of football and it's been the team's strongpoint this season.

Outside of the Mizell bombs, there weren't many explosive plays for the UCLA offense. Noah Fox-Flores broke open for a big touchdown in the first half but outside of that it was Wayne Knight. Knight scored yet another touchdown and added 109 rushing yards. Defenses have keyed in on Knight, it just hasn't mattered.

UCLA will now have a short week, hosting Illinois Friday night at home for a chance to move to 10-0. Chesney transformed a 3-9 UCLA program to a CFP-caliber team in one offseason and he's showing no signs of slowing down. UCLA could have crumbled in the fourth quarter, instead they dominated.

#2885. Former Cavs forward Dean Wade expected to be back in Cleveland this season

The Cleveland Cavaliers, like a few other teams, found themselves completely preoccupied this summer with courting LeBron James. In the process, they were unwilling or unable to give Dean Wade a financial commitment.

"I kind of thought I'd probably just come back," Wade said after playing his first game in Cleveland this season. "Unfortunately, that didn't happen."

Perhaps the Cavs would have elected to move on from Wade either way. He had run his course in Cleveland, and the Cavs clearly wanted to find an upgrade at small forward. That's certainly part of the equation.

But it also seems possible that Cleveland was holding out for LeBron — and Wade leaving was a product of that waiting game.

Regardless of how it happened, Wade says he's happy with the end result.

"Philly's awesome, this team is always great, high-character people," said Wade. "It's weird going to a new place with me, like 1,000 new faces and stuff. But after a couple of weeks, it gets a lot easier. So I'm pretty happy with my decision."

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Believeland shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cavs gear HERE.

Wade played seven seasons with the Cavaliers, going from an undrafted prospect in 2019 to a starter in the playoffs. This was his first time entering Rocket Arena as an enemy.

"It was weird; emotions were high, and it was pretty hard to stay focused," said Wade. "But it was good. I was happy to see everybody."

The Cavs leaned on Wade for his defense throughout the last seven seasons. But his progression from a fringe NBA player to a legitimate rotational piece is worth remembering. He's a developmental success story that helped bolster the Cavs for nearly a decade.

"Above all, you loved the person, loved the family; they're just incredible," Cavs coach Kenny Atkinson said of Wade. "Great for our culture, obviously the player, he's a heck of a player, helped us a lot even before I got here, a major player for the team — happy he landed in a good spot."

Wade is now playing a similar role in Philly, while the Cavs are hoping Peyton Watson will be everything they've needed at small forward. It's a breakup that has zero hard feelings on either end.

#2884. Five Washington Commanders players key to beating Rams in NFL Week 9

The Commanders are looking for their first winning streak. They've only won two games thus far, and they weren't earned in a row. While Washington simply must start stacking wins, they've been competitive while playing four quarters of football.

That will be a requirement to beat an L.A. Rams team with a so-so record but tons of star power, including quarterback Matt Stafford and defensive linemen Aaron Donald and Myles Garrett. Beating L.A. on Sunday at Northwest Stadium would secure that win streak.

All those dudes wear gold jackets someday. They're also missing some key players, including Davonte Adams and Jaylen Warren on injured reserve.

The Commanders IR list is also a who's who, with Daron Payne, Laremy Tunsil, Terry McLaurin and Javon Kinlaw unavailable now and in the long-term.

The Commanders will need important role players to step up and play huge in this one. Quarterback Jayden Daniels is the most obvious, so we'll leave him off this list, but we'll list five more players to watch in Sunday's key NFC matchup at Northwest Stadium.

Dan Quinn was frank about the Commanders giving Antonio Williams more opportunities with Terry McLaurin done for the season. That was no smokescreen. The third-round rookie led the team with seven targets during Week 8. He's playing but remains someone new to the pro game.

Stefon Diggs is the real No. 1 around here now. He has five touchdowns, but his 61.4% catch rate is the lowest of his career (just barely). While catch rate isn't exclusively a receiver stat, he and Jayden Daniels must find greater sync. Look, he won't be the dominant player of a few years back and the Commanders will spread the ball around. Diggs' leadership has been excellent. His production needs an uptick starting on Sunday.

K'Lavon Chaisson has been Washington's best edge defender this season, leading the team in overall pressures while producing against the run and pass. This is a game where quick pressures are essential and the Commanders might not blitz as much as expected considering Rams quarterback Matt Stafford's ability to beat it.

Odafe Oweh could've easily made this list, but the Commanders need an impact from both sides of the defensive front.

This is a bit of a speculative inclusion because Johnny Newton is reportedly getting activated, but hasn't been formally activated off injured reserve as of this story's publication. He has practiced for two weeks and fully heading into Week 9. Washington needs him after seeing Javon Kinlaw and Daron Payne land on season-ending injured reserve.

Newton joined the Commanders with high expectations as a 2024 second-round NFL draft pick. He hasn't lived up to them thus far, though a torn pectoral muscle from this preseason certainly set that effort back.

It's always curious how much a player returning off injured reserve can provide in terms of snap volume, but snap quality must be there. Stafford's a true pocket passer and the fastest way to disrupt those guys is through interior pressure, forcing a quick throw, flushing him from the pocket or a sack. The Commanders defensive line was good last week against Philadelphia, especially Charles Omenihu and Shy Tuttle. Adding some juice on critical downs especially could be beneficial to a team that'll need some game-changing plays.

This may seem like an odd add, including someone with just five catches for 27 yards all season, but hear us out. The Rams have some heft off the edge, especially with Myles Garrett's return off injured reserve. That man's the best edge rusher on the planet and will require extra attention. At times, it's a tight end one-on-one. That's never a good matchup for anyone.

Bates is one of the blocking tight ends around – he was dealing with a shoulder injury this week but didn't receive a designation — and could be an important part of the pass protection.

We could throw the Commanders running backs into this effort, especially with Aaron Donald working from the inside. Washington lost its best pass protecting back in Jeremy McNichols, but Rachaad White and Austin Ekeler must hold it down as the last line of defense.

Defensive coordinator Daronte Jones loves moving Nick Cross around his alignment. He's a blitzer, an active run defender in the box, a slot cornerback and a deep safety. Cross is impactful in most every area, and has been a vital addition since returning from an illness that caused hospitalization in Week 2.

The Commanders need to take the ball, set up third-and-unmanageable and then win on critical downs. Having a versatile defensive back be an unpredictable, roving chess piece should help those efforts.

Who else would you in your list of Commanders to watch during Sunday's game against L.A.? Let us know in the comments below.

#2883. Lions officially place Jack Campbell on injured reserve

The Detroit Lions on Saturday, Nov. 7, placed linebacker Jack Campbell on injured reserve.

Campbell (neck) was placed on a backboard and carted off the field in the fourth quarter of Detroit's 19-15 win over the Minnesota Vikings on Sunday, Nov. 1. Campbell, who was transported to Henry Ford Hospital, said in a statement through the team Tuesday he has "full movement in my lower body, and I'm continuing to make progress with movement in my upper body."

An official diagnosis for Campbell, 26, or a timeline for his recovery have not yet been publicly shared. In his statement, Campbell asked fans to continue praying for him and his family. "We feel the love, and I can't thank everyone enough," Campbell said.

On Friday, Lions head coach Dan Campbell said Jack Campbell "continues to improve."

"This is about, man, how would he prepare, how would he play?" Dan Campbell said. "Those are the standards for us going into this (next) game. We owe it to him."

Linebackers Derrick Barnes, Malcolm Rodriguez and Trevor Nowaske are all expected to take on larger roles on defense. Other linebackers on the active roster include Joe Bachie, Amen Ogbongbemiga and K.J. Britt, the latter of whom was signed Wednesday from the New England Patriots' practice squad.

The Lions on Saturday also signed cornerback Nick Whiteside to the active roster and temporarily elevated two players from their practice squad for Sunday's matchup with the Miami Dolphins, in defensive end Anthony Lucas and running back Jabari Small. Lucas earned a shoutout from Dan Campbell during the head coach's news conference Friday, when Campbell said the undrafted rookie from USC has been "somebody we've been repping a little bit here."

Starting defensive end D.J. Wonnum (concussion) won't play against the Dolphins. Neither will center Cade Mays (ankle), cornerback D.J. Reed or safety Kerby Joseph (knee). Juice Scruggs will replace Mays at center, and a combination of players could fill in for Reed at cornerback; options on the outside (in addition to Whiteside) include Roger McCreary, Rock Ya-Sin, Khalil Dorsey and Ennis Rakestraw Jr.

At the defensive end spot opposite of Aidan Hutchinson, Lucas, Derrick Moore and Eric O'Neill are all likely to see increased snaps. Moore has seen at least 20 reps on defense in each of his last three games. He has five pressures on the season thus far, including two quarterback hits.

#2878. In defending his willingness to spend, Jerry Jones may have admitted to tampering

On Friday, Cowboys owner Jerry Jones tried to reverse the perception/reality that he deliberately underspends on coaches. His explanation regarding the willingness to spend included an important admission.

“I have been trying to lure very successful coaches away from the teams they were on,” Jones said on 105.3 The Fan in Dallas.

Unless he was referring to college coaches (or high-school coaches), that’s tampering. Period. End of discussion.

It could be false — an effort to bolster the idea that, yes, he’s willing to spend. Still, the effort to put out the frugality fire he started on Tuesday by ridiculously claiming there’s no correlation between coaching pay and winning included a nonchalant concession that he has tried to pry NFL coaches away from their current teams, in blatant violation of the rules.

Will the NFL look into this one? Probably not. Because the league office surely realizes, as the rest of us do, that Jerry was simply saying whatever he had to say to lessen the impact of the accidental truth he told on Tuesday regarding a deliberate reluctance to do whatever he needs to do to make his team as good as it can be.

#2876. Saints release RB Kendre Miller

The New Orleans Saints have released running back Kendre Miller, per Nick Underhill.

Miller is another casualty of the Saints' 2023 draft class, which now only rosters one player (Bryan Bresee) from the four-year-old class.

Through his four-year career, Miller has logged 656 rushing yards and 5 touchdowns while adding another 217 yards as a receiver. The former third-round draft pick is also the team's current leading rusher, logging 159 rushing yards this season.

The Saints picked up running back Audric Estime off waivers heading into the bye, and it appears he will have a major role in the team's game plan on Sunday against the Cleveland Browns. Miller's release also signifies a potential return for veteran running back Travis Etienne.

The decision to move on from Miller mid-season is shocking, but the writing has been on the wall. Despite his very obvious talent, the former TCU running back has struggled to stay healthy and has made plenty of mistakes throughout the 2026 season.

There's always the chance New Orleans looks to bring Miller back to the roster or through the practice squad, but with his talent, it's unlikely he doesn't find his way on to another team's roster.

#2875. NFC North has 17-3 record against non-NFC North teams

The NFC North is the best division in the NFL. As evidenced by how the teams of the NFC North have performed against the rest of the league.

The Vikings, Bears, Lions, and Packers have a combined record of 17-3 against non-NFC North teams. Via NBC Sports research, the ensuing 85 percent success rate is the highest by any division in the NFL since the 1970 merger.

In theory, all four teams of the NFC North could make the playoffs. To do that, however, each would have to finish higher in the wild-card pecking order than the Rams and the Seahawks (assuming the 49ers win the NFC West).

This week, two teams of the NFC North have an easier path to victory than the others; the Bears host the Buccaneers and the Lions visit the Dolphins.

For the Packers (who play the Patriots in New England) and the Vikings (who play the Bills on Monday night), it will be harder to keep pushing 17-3 to 21-3.

#2874. Drew Rosenhaus after Brandon Aiyuk's meeting with 49ers: "To be continued"

It has become abundantly clear that the NFL is delaying the reinstatement of 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk from the reserve/left squad list. It has also become abundantly clear that the 49ers hope to squeeze some cash out of Aiyuk before the league lets him back in.

It’s no surprise, then, that Friday’s sit-down between Aiyuk, agent Drew Rosenhaus, and the 49ers did not resolve the situation.

“Hey, guys,” Rosenhaus told a group of reporters who had staked out the Friday meeting. “To be continued. We don’t have any further statements right now. But when we do, we’ll let you know.”

The situation seems pretty simple. Aiyuk arguably owes the 49ers upwards of $13 million. It’s one thing for the 49ers to have the ability to chase him around for the cash; it’s another for the NFL and the 49ers to delay his reinstatement to the league until he wires the funds.

That seems to be precisely where things stand. And the question becomes whether Aiyuk will be willing to strike a deal with the 49ers in order to secure the ability to return to the NFL, with another team.

"We're getting a better handle on this," Dan Campbell said. "Jack continues to improve, which is all great news and there again this is about, man, how would he prepare, how would he play? Those are the standards for us going into this game. We owe it to him."

In addition, the Lions signed defensive back Nick Whiteside to the active roster from the practice squad and elevated edge defender Anthony Lucas and running back Jabari Small from the practice squad to the active/inactive list

#2872. Saints to have "chip on our shoulder" after Browns' "chirping"

The Saints, like all teams, pay attention to the things said by their upcoming opponents.

Earlier this week, Browns quarterback Shedeur Sanders seemed to make some less-than-flattering comments about the New Orleans defense. The New Orleans defense heard it, loud and clear.

“We’re gonna come in with a chip on our shoulder, for sure,” Saints defensive end Carl Granderson said Friday. “You know, they been chirping a little bit. We heard what the quarterback . . . said. So, you know, we’re [going to] come in hot and ready to play. . . . It’s gonna be a good game, for sure. We’re gonna come out with the [win].”

Asked Wednesday by reporters whether he has had a chance to study the Saints defense and to explain what he has seen, Sanders flashed a broad smile.

“Yeah, I’ve seen a lot,” Sanders said. “When I smile, just know that means something. I’m maturing. I’m maturing. Like, I could have given a decent answer, but now I’m just going to smile whenever it’s just, like, some questions.”

The better approach for Sanders would have been to project a poker face, and to give an answer that did nothing to attract the attention of the Saints’ defense.

So while he says he’s maturing, the ultimate level of maturity would consist of providing the most vanilla answer possible, praising the Saints and explaining that it will be a major challenge to beat them.

Instead, a Browns player has provided the next opponent — for the second straight week — with a little extra incentive.

#2870. Rams listed Matthew Stafford with elbow injury because reporters asked about ice on it

On Monday, reporters asked Rams coach Sean McVay about quarterback Matthew Stafford having ice on his throwing arm after Sunday’s win over the Chargers. On Wednesday, the Rams listed Stafford as having an elbow injury.

“Really, it’s because you guys asked me about it earlier in the week,” McVay told reporters on Friday regarding the decision to disclose an elbow injury for Stafford. “It’s more semantical than anything else. It’s normal, like what he told you guys, but because you guys asked me about him having ice on his elbow. . . . He’s been full, hasn’t missed any sort of reps so normal management stuff that he’s been doing over the last few years.”

It conjures memories of the Patriots’ one-time habit of listing former quarterback Tom Brady as “probable” with a “right shoulder” injury. That all started because he had ice on his shoulder in the locker room, reporters inquired, and the team decided to just put him on the injury report every week.

Has the elbow situation affected Stafford’s throwing?

“I haven’t seen any of that,” McVay said. “I told you guys he’d be better equipped to talk about it. He’s been a full participant, been awesome. He’s spinning the ball. He’s been able to activate all parts of the field with great timing, rhythm, and accuracy so he’s been great.”

So either he has an actual elbow injury that the Rams had been concealing, or he doesn’t really have an elbow injury but they listed him as having one because reporters noticed ice on his arm and asked McVay about it. Neither explanation seems to reflect the true spirit of the injury report, which expects real injuries — and only real injuries — to be disclosed.

Either Stafford has an elbow injury that was previously hidden or he doesn’t have an elbow injury and the claim that he has one is a middle finger to the meddling reporters who were doing their jobs. Neither explanation is a preferred alternative to the unvarnished truth.

#2869. Swanson: Luka Doncic can lead Lakers to a title, but only if his teammates pull their weight

Luka Doncic is hooping like he intends to run away with the MVP trophy. Making a point by scoring more points to start a season — 361 in nine games — than anyone since Wilt Chamberlain scored 472 in 1962.

Generational scoring run or the new normal? Could this historic meteor shower be the standard?

"I feel like that," Doncic said, adding he thinks he can maintain his current 40.1-points-per-game tear. "But as long as we win, I'm good."

And if he's this good when the playoffs roll around, this undeniable, brushing off elite defenders Toumani Camara and Jrue Holiday en route to 53 points in Friday's 124-118 victory over the Portland Trail Blazers, then the Lakers could totally go on a Luka Magical run and win it all.

But if Doncic is not this good, the Lakers aren't 8-2 right now, and they're not sitting in first in the Western Conference standings out of the gate … with 72 more games to go.

There's ability, there's inevitability, bankability.

Read more: Luka Doncic scores 53 as Lakers avenge loss to Trail Blazers

The Lakers are going to need to employ some serious conservation measures to get this version of Doncic — whose seven consecutive 35-points-or-better games put him on par with Elgin Baylor and Kobe Bryant as the only Lakers to stack so many in regular-season history — to the starting line that matters.

They need to write to Santa Claus and ask for a serviceable backup center. They need Austin Reaves to really rock and roll with the units that don't include Doncic — who has the highest plus-minus and the highest on/off rating in the NBA — and to keep his turnover tallies low. They need to not just beat but blow out bad teams, to give Doncic a fourth quarter off sometimes.

And they need coach JJ Redick — who boasted in September that the Lakers' "naturally deeper roster" would result in a longer rotation — to trust the process.

Even if the amount of "my bad" from his backups costs the Lakers some regular-season wins, it will, on occasion, make more sense to punt than to go for it. Like when you're readying a less-experienced, less-familiar team for future battles. And wanting to reduce wear and tear on your engine.

A proven power-lifter, the 27-year-old Doncic is this season handling the heaviest workload (39.8% usage rate) in the NBA and in his career, while logging 35.9 taxing minutes per game.

Lakers guard Luka Doncic reacts after sinking a three-point shot to give the Lakers a five-point lead against the Trail Blazers in the third quarter Friday night at Crypto.com Arena. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

He's being challenged, pressed and trapped. He's accelerating and decelerating and accelerating again. He's charge-taking, pump-faking, piling up hoops-and-harms. Manipulating defenses and defending like he's taken some slander personally. Arguing with officials and heaving vibey buzzer-beaters from all over the building, including a 61-footer that completely altered the complexion of Friday's game, giving the Lakers momentum for the first time entering intermission.

And, no, he doesn't need to play heavy minutes to prepare to play heavy minutes in the playoffs. He also doesn't need to be wrapped in bubble wrap, but ideally he'd ramp up for the anchor leg instead of sprinting for the full marathon.

Remember what happened last season, after Doncic set the league on fire in March, averaging 37.5 points per game as the Lakers went 15-2 and announced themselves as challengers? He suffered a Grade 2 hamstring strain on April 2 that sidelined him for the rest of the season and doused the Lakers' hopes.

And look around now; the league is a cautionary tale of the tape. Anthony Edwards, Chet Holmgren, Ja Morant, Tyrese Haliburton, Jalen Williams are among the stars who've been lost to injuries.

The Lakers — who are starting the season by playing three weeks without a single two-day break between games — don't want to be included in the discussion about what's ailing the NBA's highest-usage talent this season.

It might be that the game has evolved to become more grueling, that teams are averaging more possessions per game, that possessions are shorter and that defenders are picking up ball-handlers sooner.

But it's not, Portland coach Micah Nori said, because guys aren't in shape: "They all have individual trainers, and they all work extremely hard, and they put in a lot of time — essentially 12 months out of the year. Back in the day, no disrespect to those guys, but that's why training camp was so long, because they needed training camp to get back in shape. And so now they're never out of shape, and I don't know if that has any bearing … but that's the only difference I see.

That includes Doncic. "He is the best shape I've ever seen him in," Nori said, proclaiming the Lakers' star his (way-too-early) favorite for MVP.

Shohei Ohtani being dead set on chasing the Cy Young Award killed the Dodgers' chances at a three-peat. And Doncic doing too much and earning his first MVP honor wouldn't do anything for the Lakers' championship chances if he's spent by the time the postseason starts.

Sign up for our weekly newsletter on all things Lakers.

#2868. Henrik Zetterberg says Red Wings got it wrong on Steve Yzerman removal

Henrik Zetterberg ripped his former organization over its handling of Steve Yzerman's ouster as general manager in July.

Zetterberg, an advisor for a hockey club in his native Sweden, was clear on his thoughts on the Detroit Red Wings' bizarre offseason and start to the season, with two top players – ex-captain Dylan Larkin and Simon Edvinsson – sitting out and wanting to be traded.

"It has been brutal," Zetterberg told Swedish newspaper Expressen, according to The Hockey Beast this week. "Things always happen, and there's a difference between what comes out and what doesn't, but it's sad. It was very hard to watch Steve step down.

"I'm not going to go into the reasons for it or what happened. But it was obviously very difficult, because I thought it was wrong."

Yzerman was fired as GM in July after seven years and transitioned into a senior advisory role. The Wings have the NHL's longest playoff drought at 10 seasons, and have collapsed down the stretch of multiple seasons to choke away playoff berths, including in 2025-26.

Yzerman, a Hockey Hall of Famer, was the Wings' captain when they won Stanley Cups in 1997, 1998 and 2002 and spent his entire career in the NHL in Detroit. His No. 19 jersey is retired.

The Wings, run by owner Chris Ilitch, hired a search firm to find Yzerman's replacement, but Ilitch acknowledged in October the search could last the entire 2026-27 season. The club got such a late start after removing Yzerman post-draft and free agency.

Shawn Horcoff was named interim GM in mid-September.

Zetterberg, 46, played his entire 15-year NHL career with the Wings from 2002-18, leading the team to the Stanley Cup title in 2008 with a playoff-high 13 goals and 27 points in 22 games to earn playoff MVP (Conn Smythe Trophy). He was named captain in 2013.

CARLOS: Red Wings' promise vs top teams should spark some sort of Larkin move

Larkin, 30, asked to be traded shortly after the season ended in April and was stripped of his captaincy after news broke in the summer of his trade request.

He played 10 games (with two goals and four assists) early in the season, then decided he "had enough of the situation," his agent Pat Brisson said Oct. 29. The team suspended Larkin without pay that same day, costing him $41,237.11 per day.

Larkin has a no-trade clause and is in Year 4 of an eight-year contract that is team-friendly. He has a list of 10 teams he would approve a trade to.

Edvinsson, 24 and a top-pair defenseman, has been in a contract stalemate for months. He reported to training camp, but went before the end of preseason without a new contract. Edvinsson is a restricted free agent, meaning the club must sign him by Dec. 1 or he will sit out the remainder of the season.

Zetterberg said many of the former Wings greats of the 1990s and 2000s era stay in touch, with some still employed by the Wings.

Zetterberg, who played with Yzerman for three seasons in Detroit before the latter retired in 2006, said Yzerman was the Wings' icon he was closest with, and called him the "captains' captain in my eyes."

"It's sad that it ends this way," Zetterberg said.

#2867. The Protect College Sports Act could be dead

This week’s midterm elections will dramatically reshuffle the deck in Congress. And that could keep those who want the Protect College Sports Act from having the cards necessary to get it passed.

The last, best hope for a Congressional bailout to the college sports system (at the expense of the athletes in the revenue-generating sports) comes in the upcoming six-week lame-duck session. The bill has cleared the Senate. The House of Representatives, which disbanded in the middle of September to block a push for the impeachment of U.S. Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth, has done nothing with it.

The question now becomes whether the short-timers in the House will muster the will to push their own version of the PSCA through to a successful vote.

Senator Ted Cruz (R-Texas), who co-sponsored the original bill, seems to realize that if it doesn’t happen now it never will.

“There is no realistic path to secure the same broad bipartisan support for a new bill in 2027 and beyond,” a spokesperson for the Senate Committee on Commerce, led by Cruz, told The Athletic. “Passing the Senate-approved bipartisan bill through the House and sending it to the President’s desk during the lame duck Congress gives House Republicans an opportunity to close out the 119th Congress with a significant win for President Trump, HBCUs, women’s sports, and student athletes. An attempt to amend it would be a surefire defeat for the colleges and fans counting on PCSA’s enactment.”

At this point, do House Republicans want to give Trump a win? Right or wrong, those who won’t be coming back in January surely believe they lost their bids for re-election because of him. If so, they’ll feel no compulsion to spend time and effort on another presidential pet project at a time when the list of real issues that need to be addressed keeps growing.

If anything, the House would pass an amended version that would require reconciliation while the clock on the 119th Congress ticks toward midnight. The fact that the Senate Commerce Committee regards even an attempt to amend the existing bill as a “surefire defeat” for the bill will make the current House members who soon will be former House members even more likely to say, “Why bother?”

#2866. The Vikings could be ready to make a trade for a quarterback, if they lose Monday night

Kyler Murray or Carson Wentz? Carson Wentz or Kyler Murray?

This week’s apparent soft benching of Murray, who has a hamstring injury that possibly wouldn’t have kept him from playing if the Vikings had beaten the Lions on Sunday, gives Wentz a chance to beat the 6-1 Bills on Monday night. If he pulls it off, Wentz could keep the job for the remainder of the four-game gjallarhorn gauntlet that ends with trips to Wisconsin to play the Packers and Mexico to play the 49ers.

If Wentz fails, the Vikings may mobilize. Immediately.

The Vikings will have roughly 16 hours after Monday night’s game ends to close a deal with another team for a quarterback. On Monday, we made the case for a trade for Colts quarterback Daniel Jones. As the week has unfolded, a variey of indications have emerged that it’s a real possibility.

Jones, who surely is dismayed by the Colts’ decision to prematurely throw in the towel by trading away guard Quenton Nelson and defensive tackle DeForest Bucker, would likely welcome the chance to return to a team with which he spent a couple of months in 2024 — and to lead the effort to propel the neo-Purple People Eaters to the postseason.

The other possibility would be to try to bring Kirk Cousins home. That would be more difficult. At 4-4, the Raiders are on the fringes of contention. Win or lose on Sunday at San Francisco, why disrupt something that’s working? Cousins currently ranks third in passing yards with 2,124 and second in touchdown passes with 19. His passer rating of 101.4 is higher than Josh Allen’s.

Especially since the Colts may already be planning to cut Jones before a $4 million roster bonus comes due in March and $6.51 million of his $33.49 million salary becomes fully-guaranteed.

With a new G.M. coming next year, why wouldn’t the Colts want a clean slate? Doing so would wipe out $38 million in non-guaranteed cash for 2027. Trading Jones would result in getting value; cutting Jones would get them nothing.

It may all come down to Monday night. If Minnesota wins, chances are nothing happens. If Minnesota loses, Tuesday could become a very interesting day.
